prose-gray dark:prose-invert [contain:inline-size] isolate empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Extend agents with skills" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/skills" id="content"><span data-as="p">Agent Skills extend Claude with specialized capabilities that Claude invokes when relevant. Skills are packaged as <code>SKILL.md</code> files containing instructions, descriptions, and optional supporting resources. This page also covers <a href="#commands-in-agent-sdk-sessions" class="link">commands in Agent SDK sessions</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">For more information about skills, including benefits, architecture, and authoring guidelines, see the <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/agent-skills/overview" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Agent Skills overview</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="how-skills-work-with-the-agent-sdk" aria-labelledby="_R_59deehkd74t9_"><a href="#how-skills-work-with-the-agent-sdk" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: How skills work with the Agent SDK"></a><span id="_R_59deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">How skills work with the Agent SDK</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">When using the Claude Agent SDK, skills are:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Defined as filesystem artifacts</strong>: you create each skill as a <code>SKILL.md</code> file in its own directory, such as <code>.claude/skills/&lt;name&gt;/SKILL.md</code></li>
<li><strong>Loaded from filesystem</strong>: the SDK loads skills from the filesystem locations governed by <code>settingSources</code> (TypeScript) or <code>setting_sources</code> (Python)</li>
<li><strong>Automatically discovered</strong>: once filesystem settings load, the SDK discovers skill metadata at startup from user and project directories, and loads the full content when Claude invokes the skill</li>
<li><strong>Model-invoked</strong>: Claude autonomously chooses when to use them based on context</li>
<li><strong>User-invoked</strong>: you dispatch a skill directly by sending <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> in a prompt. See <a href="#commands-in-agent-sdk-sessions" class="link">Commands in Agent SDK sessions</a></li>
<li><strong>Scoped via the <code>skills</code> option</strong>: discovered skills are enabled by default. Pass a list of skill names, <code>&quot;all&quot;</code>, or <code>[]</code> to control which skills Claude can invoke</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Unlike subagents, which you can define in the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents#programmatic-definition-recommended" class="link"><code>agents</code> option</a>, you create skills as files on disk. The SDK doesn’t provide a programmatic API for registering them.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Skills are discovered through the filesystem setting sources. With default <code>query()</code> options, the SDK loads user and project sources, so skills in <code>~/.claude/skills/</code>, <code>&lt;cwd&gt;/.claude/skills/</code>, and <code>.claude/skills/</code> in any parent directory of <code>&lt;cwd&gt;</code> up to the repository root are available. The project source also covers <code>&lt;dir&gt;/.claude/skills/</code> in each directory you pass through <code>additionalDirectories</code> (TypeScript) or <code>add_dirs</code> (Python), because the SDK passes those directories to Claude Code as <a href="/docs/en/skills#skills-from-additional-directories" class="link"><code>--add-dir</code></a>. If you set <code>settingSources</code> explicitly, include <code>&#x27;project&#x27;</code> to keep project and added-directory skills and <code>&#x27;user&#x27;</code> to keep your personal skills, or use the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/plugins" class="link"><code>plugins</code> option</a> to load skills from a specific path.</span></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="use-skills-with-the-agent-sdk" aria-labelledby="_R_f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#use-skills-with-the-agent-sdk" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Use skills with the Agent SDK"></a><span id="_R_f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Use skills with the Agent SDK</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Set the <code>skills</code> option on <code>query()</code> to control which skills Claude can invoke in the main conversation. When omitted, discovered skills are enabled and the Skill tool is available, matching CLI behavior. Pass <code>&quot;all&quot;</code> to let Claude invoke every discovered skill, a list of skill names to allow only those, or <code>[]</code> to let Claude invoke none.</span>
<span data-as="p">For example, to let Claude invoke only two named skills:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 min-w-0 bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/5 dark:codeblock-dark text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 relative pr-2.5" <h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="set-up-skills-in-a-session" aria-labelledby="_R_n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#set-up-skills-in-a-session" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Set up skills in a session"></a><span id="_R_n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Set up skills in a session</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When you set <code>skills</code>, the SDK adds the skills you enable to <code>allowedTools</code> automatically. If you also pass an explicit <code>tools</code> list, include <code>&quot;Skill&quot;</code> in that list so Claude can invoke skills.</span>
<span data-as="p">Once configured, Claude automatically discovers skills from the filesystem and invokes them when relevant to the user’s request.</span>
<span data-as="p">The following example enables every discovered skill in a session and pre-approves the tools that skills commonly need. The example sets <code>cwd</code> to the process’s current working directory, so run it from inside a project that has a <code>.claude/skills/</code> directory in the current directory or any parent up to the repository root:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 min-w-0 bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/5 dark:codeblock-dark text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 relative pr-2.5" data-component-part="code-group-tab-bar"><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" class="min-w-0 flex-1 w-0 rounded-tl-xl"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_58v9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! <h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="confirm-skills-loaded" aria-labelledby="_R_119deehkd74t9_"><a href="#confirm-skills-loaded" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Confirm skills loaded"></a><span id="_R_119deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Confirm skills loaded</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Near the start of the stream, the SDK yields a system message with subtype <code>init</code>. Check its <code>skills</code> array to confirm your skills loaded before Claude starts working. The array includes the user-invocable skills that you have defined with a <code>description</code> or <code>when_to_use</code> frontmatter field, along with <a href="/docs/en/skills#bundled-skills" class="link">bundled skills included with Claude Code</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">The array lists user-invocable skills only. A skill with <a href="/docs/en/skills#control-who-invokes-a-skill" class="link"><code>user-invocable: false</code></a> in its frontmatter loads and remains available to Claude, but doesn’t appear in the array. The array lists the same skills whether or not they’re in your <code>skills</code> list.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="allow-only-specific-skills" aria-labelledby="_R_179deehkd74t9_"><a href="#allow-only-specific-skills" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Allow only specific skills"></a><span id="_R_179deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Allow only specific skills</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">To let Claude invoke only specific skills, pass their names in the <code>skills</code> list:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Your own skills</strong>: the skill’s directory name</li>
<li><strong>Plugin skills</strong>: <code>plugin:skill</code></li>
<li><strong>Name clashes in <code>additionalDirectories</code> or <code>add_dirs</code></strong>: a skill in one of those directories gets a <a href="/docs/en/skills#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name" class="link">prefixed name</a> when its name clashes with an enterprise, personal, or project skill, or one in a directory added earlier. List it by that name. With a <code>verify</code> skill in <code>../api</code> and <code>../web</code>, added in that order, and no other <code>verify</code> skill, list them as <code>verify</code> and <code>web:verify</code>. Requires Claude Code v2.1.309 or later</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The list takes exact skill names only. If an entry can’t work as an exact name, <code>query()</code> rejects the list before the session starts. See <a href="#invalid-skill-name-error" class="link">Invalid skill name error</a> for the name rules and the error each SDK raises.</span>
<span data-as="p">The model doesn’t see unlisted skills and the Skill tool rejects them, while their files remain on disk and stay reachable through Read and Bash. Restricting the list doesn’t restrict subagents or <a href="#dispatch-commands-by-name" class="link">dispatch by name</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">To let Claude invoke every discovered skill, pass <code>skills: &quot;all&quot;</code> rather than a wildcard.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="commands-in-agent-sdk-sessions" aria-labelledby="_R_1j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#commands-in-agent-sdk-sessions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Commands in Agent SDK sessions"></a><span id="_R_1j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Commands in Agent SDK sessions</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">This section is the SDK’s command documentation. A command is anything you run by sending <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> in a prompt. Entries on the command surface differ in what backs them:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Built-in commands</strong>: execute logic coded into the Claude Code process the SDK runs, for example <code>/compact</code></li>
<li><strong>Bundled skills</strong>: prompt artifacts included with Claude Code, for example <code>/code-review</code></li>
<li><strong>Your skills</strong>: prompt artifacts that you author, each a directory holding a <code>SKILL.md</code> file. A user-invocable skill’s name joins the surface automatically, so dispatching your own <code>/security-check</code> and running a built-in work the same way</li>
<li><strong>Custom command files</strong>: an older artifact form with the same behavior, flat Markdown files in <code>.claude/commands/</code> whose filenames become command names. Skills are their recommended successor</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">By default, both you and Claude can invoke any skill. You can restrict either path through the skill’s <a href="/docs/en/skills#control-who-invokes-a-skill" class="link">frontmatter</a>. For definitions of command and skill, see the glossary’s <a href="/docs/en/glossary#command" class="link">Command</a> and <a href="/docs/en/glossary#skill" class="link">Skill</a> entries. See <a href="/docs/en/commands" class="link">Commands in Claude Code</a> for every built-in and <a href="/docs/en/skills" class="link">Extend Claude with skills</a> for the complete guide to both artifact forms.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="discover-available-commands" aria-labelledby="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#discover-available-commands" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Discover available commands"></a><span id="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Discover available commands</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">You can dispatch commands that work without an interactive terminal through the SDK. The <code>system/init</code> message lists the ones available in your session in its <code>slash_commands</code> field. Commands that need an interactive terminal, such as <code>/theme</code> and <code>/terminal-setup</code>, don’t appear in the list. Access the field when your session starts:</span>

<span data-as="p">A skill with <a href="/docs/en/skills#control-who-invokes-a-skill" class="link"><code>user-invocable: false</code></a> in its frontmatter doesn’t appear in this list or in the <code>skills</code> array from <a href="#confirm-skills-loaded" class="link">Confirm skills loaded</a>. Sessions that configure <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp" class="link">MCP servers</a> can also expose <a href="/docs/en/mcp#use-mcp-prompts-as-commands" class="link">MCP prompts as commands</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="dispatch-commands-by-name" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#dispatch-commands-by-name" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Dispatch commands by name"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Dispatch commands by name</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Send a command by including it in your prompt string, the same way you send regular text. Dispatch doesn’t depend on the <code>skills</code> option. Sending <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> runs a user-invocable skill even when your <code>skills</code> list omits it. Commands that act on conversation history, such as <code>/compact</code>, need prior messages to work with.</span>
<span data-as="p">A <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> that matches neither a command in the session nor a built-in Claude Code command doesn’t fail the query. Claude Code sends the prompt to Claude as an ordinary message, with a note that the command didn’t run, so the query spends a model turn and returns Claude’s reply. Before v2.1.274, a <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> that matched nothing returned <code>Unknown command: /&lt;name&gt;</code> as the result without a model turn.</span>
<span data-as="p">A <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> that matches a built-in Claude Code command that isn’t available in the session, such as <code>/theme</code>, returns <code>/theme isn&#x27;t available in this environment.</code> as the result without a model turn.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">When a command reaches the <code>maxTurns</code> / <code>max_turns</code> limit, the query ends with an error result instead of <code>success</code>. For the error-result contract, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop#handle-the-result" class="link">Handle the result</a>. If your command might hit the limit, do one of these:</span><ul>
<li>Set <code>maxTurns</code> high enough for the work to complete.</li>
<li>Wrap the <code>query()</code> loop in <code>try</code>/<code>catch</code> in TypeScript or <code>try</code>/<code>except</code> in Python.</li>
</ul></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="compact-history-with-/compact" aria-labelledby="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#compact-history-with-/compact" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Compact history with /compact"></a><span id="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Compact history with <code>/compact</code></span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The <code>/compact</code> command reduces the size of your conversation history by summarizing older messages while preserving important context. Compaction needs an existing conversation with enough prior messages to summarize. This example has a conversation first, then compacts it and reads the <code>compact_boundary</code> system message that reports the result:</span>

<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">A <code>compact_boundary</code> message only arrives when compaction ran. When a continued session has messages but nothing <code>/compact</code> can summarize, the run still ends with a <code>success</code> result rather than an error, and no <code>compact_boundary</code> message arrives. The result text then carries the reason, for example <code>Not enough messages to compact.</code> when the session holds a prompt but no reply from Claude yet. A fresh one-shot <code>query()</code> call starts with empty context, so use this pattern in a session with prior turns, for example in <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode" class="link">streaming input mode</a> or when resuming a session.</span></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="reset-context-with-/clear" aria-labelledby="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#reset-context-with-/clear" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Reset context with /clear"></a><span id="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Reset context with <code>/clear</code></span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The <code>/clear</code> command resets the conversation to an empty context, so subsequent prompts start with no prior conversation history. The previous conversation remains on disk. You can return to that conversation by passing its session ID to the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/sessions#resume-by-id" class="link"><code>resume</code> option</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p"><code>/clear</code> is useful in <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode" class="link">streaming input mode</a>, where you send multiple prompts over a single connection. For one-shot <code>query()</code> calls, each call already starts with empty context, so sending <code>/clear</code> has no practical effect. Start a new <code>query()</code> instead.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="create-skills" aria-labelledby="_R_2v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#create-skills" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Create skills"></a><span id="_R_2v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Create skills</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Create each skill as a directory containing a <code>SKILL.md</code> file with YAML frontmatter and Markdown content. The <code>description</code> field determines when Claude invokes your skill.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Example directory structure</strong>:</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="choose-a-discovery-level" aria-labelledby="_R_379deehkd74t9_"><a href="#choose-a-discovery-level" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Choose a discovery level"></a><span id="_R_379deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Choose a discovery level</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Save skills at either of the two most common <a href="/docs/en/skills#where-skills-live" class="link">discovery levels</a>:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Project skills</strong>: <code>.claude/skills/</code>, available only in the current project</li>
<li><strong>Personal skills</strong>: <code>~/.claude/skills/</code>, available across all your projects</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">If you have existing custom command files in <code>.claude/commands/</code>, they keep working. A command file at <code>.claude/commands/deploy.md</code> creates <code>/deploy</code> and works the same way as a skill at <code>.claude/skills/deploy/SKILL.md</code> would. If a command file and a skill share a name, see <a href="/docs/en/skills#resolve-skills-that-share-a-name" class="link">Resolve skills that share a name</a> for which one runs. The SDK loads <code>.claude/commands/</code> and <code>~/.claude/commands/</code> files from the same two scopes as skills. See <a href="/docs/en/skills" class="link">Extend Claude with skills</a> for the complete guide to both artifact forms.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="create-and-dispatch-your-first-skill" aria-labelledby="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#create-and-dispatch-your-first-skill" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Create and dispatch your first skill"></a><span id="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Create and dispatch your first skill</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">To see the full flow, create <code>.claude/skills/security-check/SKILL.md</code>:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4" language="text"><code language="text" numberOfLines="5"><span class="line"><span>**Security scan of `app.js` — 4 findings (most severe first):**</span></span>
<span class="line"><span></span></span>
<span class="line"><span>1. **SQL Injection** (line 8) — `req.query.name` is concatenated directly into the SQL string. Trivially exploitable (`&#x27; OR &#x27;1&#x27;=&#x27;1`, `&#x27;; DROP TABLE users;--`). **Fix:** use parameterized queries, e.g. `db.query(&quot;SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = ?&quot;, [req.query.name], cb)`.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>...</span></span>
<span class="line"><span></span></span></code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The skill’s name also appears in the init message’s <code>slash_commands</code> array.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Claude Code includes bundled <code>code-review</code> and <code>verify</code> skills. If you name a <code>.claude/commands/</code> file after one of them, for example <code>.claude/commands/code-review.md</code>, the file’s command shadows the bundled skill and <code>slash_commands</code> lists the name once.</span></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="pre-approve-tools-for-skills" aria-labelledby="_R_419deehkd74t9_"><a href="#pre-approve-tools-for-skills" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Pre-approve tools for skills"></a><span id="_R_419deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Pre-approve tools for skills</span></h2>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">In SDK sessions, you can pre-approve tools for a project or personal skill with the skill’s <a href="/docs/en/skills#pre-approve-tools-for-a-skill" class="link"><code>allowed-tools</code></a> frontmatter or with the <code>allowedTools</code> option (<code>allowed_tools</code> in Python) in your query configuration. If your organization sets <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#allowmanagedpermissionrulesonly" class="link"><code>allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly</code></a> in managed settings, Claude Code ignores both. Skills <a href="/docs/en/skills#how-claude-code-handles-the-frontmatter-of-a-synced-skill" class="link">synced from claude.ai</a> follow their own frontmatter rules.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Skills run with the session’s tools. The example below pre-approves <code>Read</code>, <code>Grep</code>, and <code>Glob</code> with <code>allowedTools</code> (<code>allowed_tools</code> in Python), so Claude can inspect files while running the <a href="#create-and-dispatch-your-first-skill" class="link">security-check skill</a> without stopping for approval:</span>

<span data-as="p">In the stream, the skill invocation appears as a Skill tool use, followed by Read calls on the project files. The run ends with a <code>success</code> result whose text carries the findings.</span>
<span data-as="p">The list pre-approves the named tools rather than restricting the others. For the full permission flow, including permission modes and the <code>canUseTool</code> callback, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">Permissions</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="troubleshooting" aria-labelledby="_R_4d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#troubleshooting" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Troubleshooting"></a><span id="_R_4d9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Troubleshooting</span></h2>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="skills-not-found" aria-labelledby="_R_4f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#skills-not-found" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Skills not found"></a><span id="_R_4f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Skills not found</span></h3>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Check settingSources configuration</strong>: the SDK discovers skills through the <code>user</code> and <code>project</code> setting sources. If you set <code>settingSources</code>/<code>setting_sources</code> explicitly and omit those sources, the SDK doesn’t load skills:</span>

<span data-as="p">For which skill directories each source loads, see the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features#control-filesystem-settings-with-settingsources" class="link">filesystem sources table</a>. For more details on <code>settingSources</code>/<code>setting_sources</code>, see the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#settingsource" class="link">TypeScript SDK reference</a> or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#settingsource" class="link">Python SDK reference</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Check working directory</strong>: the SDK loads skills from <code>.claude/skills/</code> in the <code>cwd</code> option and in every parent directory up to the repository root. Ensure <code>cwd</code> points at or below the directory containing <code>.claude/skills/</code>, within the same repository:</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="skill-not-being-used" aria-labelledby="_R_519deehkd74t9_"><a href="#skill-not-being-used" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Skill not being used"></a><span id="_R_519deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Skill not being used</span></h3>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Check the <code>skills</code> option</strong>: if you passed a <code>skills</code> list, confirm the skill’s name is included. When Claude tries to invoke an unlisted skill, the Skill tool returns <code>Skill &lt;name&gt; is not in this session&#x27;s skills allowlist</code>. Add the name to your list, or dispatch the skill directly by sending <code>/&lt;name&gt;</code> in a prompt, which works without listing.</span>
<span data-as="p"><strong>Check the description</strong>: ensure it’s specific and includes relevant keywords. See <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/agent-skills/best-practices#writing-effective-descriptions" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Agent Skills best practices</a> for guidance on writing effective descriptions.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="invalid-skill-name-error" aria-labelledby="_R_579deehkd74t9_"><a href="#invalid-skill-name-error" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Invalid skill name error"></a><span id="_R_579deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Invalid skill name error</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When a name in your <code>skills</code> list can’t work as an exact skill name, <code>query()</code> rejects the list before starting the Claude Code process. Names that trigger the rejection include:</span>
<ul>
<li>An empty name</li>
<li>A name containing parentheses, commas, or control characters</li>
<li>A name padded with whitespace</li>
<li>A wildcard form such as a bare <code>*</code> or a <code>:*</code> suffix</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Each SDK surfaces the rejection differently:</span>
<div class="tabs tabs tab-container"><ul role="tablist" class="not-prose mb-6 pb-[1px] flex-none min-w-full overflow-auto border-b border-gray-200 gap-x-6 flex dark:border-gray-200/10" data-component-part="tabs-list"><li id="typescript" role="tab" aria-selected="true" aria-controls="panel-typescript-0" tabindex="0" class="cursor-pointer"><div class="flex text-sm items-center gap-1.5 leading-6 font-semibold whitespace-nowrap pt-3 pb-2.5 -mb-px max-w-max border-b text-primary dark:text-primary-light border-current" data-component-part="tab-button" data-active="true" data-testid="tab-TypeScript"> <!-- -->TypeScript</div></li><li id="python" role="tab" aria-selected="false" aria-controls="panel-python-1" tabindex="-1" class="cursor-pointer"><div class="flex text-sm items-center gap-1.5 leading-6 font-semibold whitespace-nowrap pt-3 pb-2.5 -mb-px max-w-max border-b text-gray-900 border-transparent hover:border-gray-300 dark:text-gray-200 dark:hover:border-gray-700" data-component-part="tab-button" data-active="false" data-testid="tab-Python"> <!-- -->Python</div></li></ul><div><div id="panel-typescript-0" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="typescript" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 -ml-4 pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0!" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">The TypeScript SDK throws an <code>Error</code> stating the rule the entry broke. <h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="additional-troubleshooting" aria-labelledby="_R_5h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#additional-troubleshooting" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Additional troubleshooting"></a><span id="_R_5h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Additional troubleshooting</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">For general skills troubleshooting, such as YAML syntax errors and debugging, see the <a href="/docs/en/skills#troubleshooting" class="link">Claude Code skills troubleshooting section</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="next-steps" aria-labelledby="_R_5l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#next-steps" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Next steps"></a><span id="_R_5l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Next steps</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The <a href="/docs/en/skills" class="link">Claude Code skills guide</a> covers authoring in depth. Its guidance applies to SDK sessions. Start with these sections:</span>
<ul>
<li><a href="/docs/en/skills#frontmatter-reference" class="link">Frontmatter reference</a>: every supported field</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/skills#pass-arguments-to-skills" class="link">Pass arguments to skills</a>: <code>$ARGUMENTS</code>, <code>$0</code>, and <code>$1</code>. The <a href="/docs/en/skills#available-string-substitutions" class="link">full substitution table</a> adds named arguments and the <code>${CLAUDE_*}</code> variables</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/skills#inject-dynamic-context" class="link">Inject dynamic context</a>: <code>!`command`</code> lines that run before Claude sees the skill content</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/skills#where-skills-live" class="link">Choose where skills load</a>: every skill location, plugin namespacing, and which skill runs when two share a name</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="related-resources" aria-labelledby="_R_5r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#related-resources" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Related resources"></a><span id="_R_5r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Related resources</span></h2>

<li><a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/agent-skills/best-practices" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Agent Skills best practices</a>: authoring guidelines for effective skills</li>
<li><a href="https://platform.claude.com/cookbook/skills-notebooks-01-skills-introduction" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Agent Skills cookbook</a>: example skills and templates</li>

The SDK doesn’t provide a programmatic API for registering them.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Note,{children:_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Skills are discovered through the filesystem setting sources. The project source also covers `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\u003cdir>/.claude/skills/`}),` in each directory you pass through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`additionalDirectories`}),` (TypeScript) or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`add_dirs`}),` (Python), because the SDK passes those directories to Claude Code as `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#skills-from-additional-directories`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--add-dir`})}),`. skills loaded`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Near the start of the stream, the SDK yields a system message with subtype `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`init`}),`. Check its `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` array to confirm your skills loaded before Claude starts working. The array includes the user-invocable skills that you have defined with a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`description`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`when_to_use`}),` frontmatter field, along with `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#bundled-skills`,children:`bundled skills included with Claude Code`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The array lists user-invocable skills only. A skill with `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#control-who-invokes-a-skill`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`user-invocable: false`})}),` in its frontmatter loads and remains available to Claude, but doesn’t appear in the array. The array lists the same skills whether or not they’re in your `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` list.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`allow-only-specific-skills`,children:`Allow only specific skills`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To let Claude invoke only specific skills, pass their names in the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` list:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Your own skills`}),`: the skill’s directory name`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Plugin skills`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`plugin:skill`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[`Name clashes in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`additionalDirectories`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`add_dirs`})]}),`: a skill in one of those directories gets a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name`,children:`prefixed name`}),` when its name clashes with an enterprise, personal, or project skill, or one in a directory added earlier. List it by that name. With a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`verify`}),` skill in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`../api`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`../web`}),`, added in that order, and no other `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`verify`}),` skill, list them as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`verify`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`web:verify`}),`. Requires Claude Code v2.1.309 or later`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The list takes exact skill names only. If an entry can’t work as an exact name, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` rejects the list before the session starts. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#invalid-skill-name-error`,children:`Invalid skill name error`}),` for the name rules and the error each SDK raises.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The model doesn’t see unlisted skills and the Skill tool rejects them, while their files remain on disk and stay reachable through Read and Bash. Restricting the list doesn’t restrict subagents or `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#dispatch-commands-by-name`,children:`dispatch by name`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To let Claude invoke every discovered skill, pass `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills: \"all\"`}),` rather than a wildcard.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`commands-in-agent-sdk-sessions`,children:`Commands in Agent SDK sessions`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`This section is the SDK’s command documentation. A command is anything you run by sending `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/\u003cname>`}),` in a prompt. Entries on the command surface differ in what backs them:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Built-in commands`}),`: execute logic coded into the Claude Code process the SDK runs, for example `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Bundled skills`}),`: prompt artifacts included with Claude Code, for example `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/code-review`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Your skills`}),`: prompt artifacts that you author, each a directory holding a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SKILL.md`}),` file. A user-invocable skill’s name joins the surface automatically, so dispatching your own `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/security-check`}),` and running a built-in work the same way`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Custom command files`}),`: an older artifact form with the same behavior, flat Markdown files in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`.claude/commands/`}),` whose filenames become command names. Skills are their recommended successor`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`By default, both you and Claude can invoke any skill. You can restrict either path through the skill’s `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#control-who-invokes-a-skill`,children:`frontmatter`}),`. For definitions of command and skill, see the glossary’s `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/glossary#command`,children:`Command`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/glossary#skill`,children:`Skill`}),` entries. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/commands`,children:`Commands in Claude Code`}),` for every built-in and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills`,children:`Extend Claude with skills`}),` for the complete guide to both artifact forms.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`discover-available-commands`,children:`Discover available commands`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`You can dispatch commands that work without an interactive terminal through the SDK. Commands that need an interactive terminal, such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/theme`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/terminal-setup`}),`, don’t appear in the list. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` array from `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#confirm-skills-loaded`,children:`Confirm skills loaded`}),`. Sessions that configure `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/mcp`,children:`MCP servers`}),` can also expose `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#use-mcp-prompts-as-commands`,children:`MCP prompts as commands`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`dispatch-commands-by-name`,children:`Dispatch commands by name`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Send a command by including it in your prompt string, the same way you send regular text. Dispatch doesn’t depend on the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` option. Sending `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/\u003cname>`}),` runs a user-invocable skill even when your `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`skills`}),` list omits it. Commands that act on conversation history, such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),`, need prior messages to work with.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`A `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/\u003cname>`}),` that matches neither a command in the session nor a built-in Claude Code command doesn’t fail the query. Claude Code sends the prompt to Claude as an ordinary message, with a note that the command didn’t run, so the query spends a model turn and returns Claude’s reply. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`compact_boundary`}),` message only arrives when compaction ran. When a continued session has messages but nothing `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),` can summarize, the run still ends with a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`success`}),` result rather than an error, and no `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`compact_boundary`}),` message arrives. The result text then carries the reason, for example `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Not enough messages to compact.`}),` when the session holds a prompt but no reply from Claude yet. A fresh one-shot `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call starts with empty context, so use this pattern in a session with prior turns, for example in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode`,children:`streaming input mode`}),` or when resuming a session.`]})}),`\n`,_jsxs(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`reset-context-with-/clear`,children:[`Reset context with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` command resets the conversation to an empty context, so subsequent prompts start with no prior conversation history. The previous conversation remains on disk. You can return to that conversation by passing its session ID to the `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/sessions#resume-by-id`,children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`resume`}),` option`]}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` is useful in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode`,children:`streaming input mode`}),`, where you send multiple prompts over a single connection. For one-shot `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` calls, each call already starts with empty context, so sending `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` has no practical effect. Start a new `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` instead.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`create-skills`,children:`Create skills`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Create each skill as a directory containing a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SKILL.md`}),` file with YAML frontmatter and Markdown content.
